# Management-Governance Module — AKAM (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Akamai’s leaders look competent but weakly aligned. Capital deployment has not proven per-share value creation: FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m+$872.1m+$106.2m+$434.1m+$55.1m=$2.066bn, and H1 buybacks of $615.744m were 278% of $221.174m cash after total capex. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.7] Pay disclosure is incomplete; 94.4% of available grants were service-based, founder-CEO ownership was 1.9%, and observed sales were plan-driven. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18, pp.84–86; Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified] The largest risk is that repeated acquisitions, including another $205m for LayerX, lack disclosed deal-level returns. [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 6, p.17] The verdict is misaligned or weak stewardship, with no hard disqualifier but material evidence gaps.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Misaligned or weak stewardship
- **NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE: PASS** — 116 of 179 checklist items were answered. No hard disqualifier, Critical red flag, Disqualifying-graded controller/CEO/CFO/chair, or material undisclosed legal matter was found in the completed, coverage-limited work.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — “No disqualifier triggered.” [Business-Model Disqualifier Scan, AKAM, Verdict-Lock Signal, 2026-09-14]
- Reporting regime: U.S. issuer listed on Nasdaq, U.S. GAAP, fiscal year ending December 31. [FY2025 Form 10-K, cover and Item 8, filed 2026-02-20]
- Management quality /100: **56**
- Capital allocation /100: **38**
- Incentive alignment /100: **43**
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **60**
- Disclosure candor /100: **54**
- People & network integrity /100: **59**
- Audit & assurance quality /100: **51**
- RPT (related-party transaction) & leakage risk /100 **(higher = worse): 45**
- Contingent-liability risk /100 **(higher = worse): 37**
- Accounting-forensics risk /100 **(higher = worse): 38**
- Legal & regulatory risk /100 **(higher = worse): 40** — the specialist’s 32 was raised to the mandatory 40 floor because the legal-database sweep was coverage-limited.
- Governance risk /100 **(higher = worse): 60** — the pre-cap synthesis read was 50 because the six fired flags are High rather than Critical and no controller/integrity lock was found; the serial-acquirer rule raises it to its 60 floor.
- Data quality /100: **58** — 16 source files and 38 workbook tabs had no extraction failure, but the frozen evidence omits the proxy, compensation, complete ownership/insider, RPT, voting, and auditor-fee records. [Governance Data Triage, AKAM, §§1 and 5–6, 2026-09-14]
- Overall usefulness /100: **68** — all expanded specialists ran and the 179-item registry is complete, but the no-proxy cap limits usefulness to 70.
- **Checklist coverage:** **116/179 (64.8%)** — 40 Green / 67 Amber / 9 Red / 63 not available or insufficient.
- Insider ownership (one line): Founder-CEO F. Thomson Leighton held 1.9%, and all directors/officers held 2.3%, as of 2026-02-20; 19,600 observed open-market shares were sold under Rule 10b5-1 plans, no observed open-market purchase was found, and the complete record and actual pledge balance remain unavailable. [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, Security Ownership, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified public SEC filing supplement; Web: SEC Forms 4, 2025-11-07 to 2026-07-15 — unverified public filing sweep]
- Biggest governance signal (one line): FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m + $872.1m + $106.2m + $434.1m + $55.1m = $2.066bn, another $205m went to LayerX without a disclosed deal-level return record, and H1 buybacks of $615.744m were 278.4% of $221.174m cash from operations after total capex while shares outstanding fell by only 1.120m, or 0.8%. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 6, p.17, Statements of Cash Flows, p.7, and Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12]
- **Checklist Risk = max(45, 37, 38, 40) = 45**, set by RPT & leakage risk; the missing RPT ledger prevents a low-risk conclusion.
- **Governance Score /100 = 50** (unrounded 50.29) = 0.14×38 + 0.11×43 + 0.11×60 + 0.10×54 + 0.11×56 + 0.09×51 + 0.11×59 + 0.11×(100−60) + 0.12×(100−45).
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100 = 33** (unrounded 32.69) = 50.29 × 65 / 100.
- **Governance Rating:** Weak
- **Confidence Score /100:** 65 — audited/current filings support the financial and audit facts, while the public-proxy supplement and incomplete legal, registry, sanctions, and ownership coverage prevent a higher score.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** **6 / 0**

Free cash flow (FCF) means cash from operations (CFO) minus total capex. “Strict” net debt means total debt minus cash equivalents; net debt/EBITDA divides that figure by earnings before interest, tax, depreciation, and amortization. Earnings per share (EPS) is profit allocated per diluted share; GAAP is the reported accounting basis, while non-GAAP removes company-selected items. Stock-based compensation (SBC) is employee pay settled in shares or share-linked awards; profit after tax (PAT) is bottom-line profit, and key management personnel (KMP) are the senior people who direct the company.

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m + $872.1m + $106.2m + $434.1m + $55.1m = $2.066bn, LayerX added $205m, and the admitted evidence contains no deal-by-deal return on invested capital (ROIC—the profit earned per dollar invested), synergy-delivery, impairment, or alternative-use comparison; H1 buybacks of $615.744m were 278.4% of $221.174m strict FCF while shares outstanding fell by 1.120m, or 0.8%. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 6, p.17, Statements of Cash Flows, p.7, and Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12]
- **Strongest bull point:** Q2 2026 revenue, non-GAAP EPS, and non-GAAP margin all landed inside management’s prior ranges; H1 repurchases reduced shares outstanding by 1.120 million, and the FY2025 audit and internal-control-over-financial-reporting (ICFR) opinions were unqualified. [Q1 FY2026 earnings release, Financial Guidance, p.2; Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Results, p.1; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12; FY2025 Form 10-K, pp.51–52, 94]
- **Single killer risk:** Capital continues to move into acquisitions, cloud infrastructure, and buybacks without returns above the cost of capital, leaving minority holders with higher leverage and no proven per-share value gain.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** H1 share count fell 0.8%, the new convertible notes have zero coupons, and CIQ reported 32.6× LTM EBITDA/interest coverage; these facts weaken an immediate financial-distress interpretation but do not establish sound capital returns. [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12 and Note 7, pp.17–20; ciq_facts.json, interest_coverage_x, 2026-09-14 — Tier-5 vendor]

## 3. Reconciliation

- **Dedicated capital-allocation work supersedes the quick read.** The business-model quick read scored capital allocation at 45 and did not apply the serial-acquirer cap. The dedicated specialist examined the five-year cash-acquisition record, LayerX, buyback funding, filed convertible principal, return-on-capital trend, and missing deal returns; its 38 score and RF-CAP-004 therefore govern this synthesis. [Business-Model Capital Allocation Governance, AKAM, 2026-09-14; Capital Allocation Scorecard, AKAM, §§1–5, 2026-09-14]
- **Ownership alignment is not the same as shareholder-rights structure.** Agent 04 refused to assign a numerical ownership-alignment score because the complete transaction and pledge data are absent. Agent 05 could assess a public-proxy board and rights structure, but its raw 67 was capped at 60. This synthesis uses 60 for shareholder friendliness while preserving ownership alignment as insufficient.
- **The subsidiary count is 46, not 57.** Agent 07 initially counted 57 entries. Agent 09 re-counted the primary Exhibit 21.1 and found 46 legal entities; the primary-source count wins, with no omission inferred. [Related-Party & Group Forensics, AKAM, finding 09-019, 2026-09-14]
- **Noname identity is resolved, not cleared.** Agent 07 left Noname Gate Ltd. and “Noname Security” pending. Agent 12 matched the latter to the acquired business/trade label of Noname Gate, not a predecessor of Akamai; Israeli registry, primary court, and sanctions coverage remain incomplete, so neither row becomes resolved-clean. [Regulatory, Legal & Compliance, AKAM, §1A, 2026-09-14]
- **The legal sweep adds a sub-material case without changing the contingency conclusion.** Agent 10 had filings-only litigation coverage. Agent 12 found a secondary-source report of the $3.741 million Barkan claim, 0.079% of Q2 equity, but no primary docket; it stays Amber and does not trigger RF-CMP-001. [Regulatory, Legal & Compliance, AKAM, §§1A and 8, 2026-09-14]
- **Candor’s filing-timeliness input remains unproven.** Agent 06 used a neutral placeholder because agent 12 had not yet completed its work; agent 12 later verified only one of the required 12 quarters. The 54 candor score is retained, but no positive timeliness claim is carried.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

Management has a stable operating bench and met the one completed quarterly guide, but a single interval cannot prove a durable promises-versus-delivery record. [Management & Track Record, AKAM, §§2–5, 2026-09-14] Capital use has not yet shown per-share value creation: acquisitions lack disclosed return scorecards, buybacks exceeded strict free cash flow, GAAP diluted EPS fell from $3.52 in FY2023 to $3.07 in FY2025, and return on capital declined on the vendor series. [Capital Allocation Scorecard, AKAM, §§1–5, 2026-09-14] The available incentive evidence points more toward retention and size than per-share returns, while 1.9% founder-CEO ownership gives exposure without control and the complete ownership record is missing. [Incentives & Compensation, AKAM, §§2–4; Ownership & Insider Behavior, AKAM, §§1–5, 2026-09-14] Minority holders have an independent chair and one-share/one-vote structure, but the 41.9% stock-plan opposition, conditional single-trigger vesting, takeover limits, and adjusted-earnings emphasis weaken protection. [Board & Shareholder Rights, AKAM, §§1–5; Candor & Disclosure Quality, AKAM, §§2–5, 2026-09-14] The main reason not to entrust more capital without proof is the combination of repeated acquisitions, rising leverage, and no deal-level return record. [Capital Allocation Scorecard, AKAM, §5, 2026-09-14]

## 6. What Would Change The Stewardship Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Misaligned or weak stewardship | Two to three years of deal-level returns above the cost of capital; strict FCF covering buybacks; GAAP EPS and return on capital improving; lower equity burn; a board response to the 41.9% dissent; full legal/person checks without adverse findings | Another debt-funded acquisition without a return hurdle; impairments or divestitures exposing overpayment; strict leverage staying above 5×; buybacks continuing above FCF; widening GAAP-adjusted gaps; a material undisclosed matter or adverse person finding | First: frozen FY2026 DEF 14A. Then deal scorecards, complete Form 4/13D/13G and pledge data, current Form AP and fee table, intercompany/RPT ledger, official legal/registry/sanctions results, and the next annual lease schedule |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- Treat the stewardship verdict as **misaligned or weak**: FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m + $872.1m + $106.2m + $434.1m + $55.1m = $2.066bn, and LayerX added $205m, without disclosed deal-level returns.
- **Non-Negotiable Gate: PASS, with 116/179 checklist items answered.** No hard disqualifier, Critical flag, Disqualifying controller/KMP, or material undisclosed legal matter was confirmed.
- Capital deployment has not proven per-share value creation: H1 share count fell, but buybacks were 278.4% of strict FCF, GAAP diluted EPS fell from $3.52 to $3.07 in FY2023–FY2025, and vendor return on capital fell.
- Incentives and ownership do not prove alignment with minority holders: 94.42% of FY2025 grant units were service-based, burn was 4.73%, conditional single-trigger vesting exists, and founder-CEO ownership was 1.9%.
- **People read:** 24 Clean, coverage-limited; 0 Minor, 0 Material, 0 Disqualifying; one unnamed CSO. Aaron Ahola is the clearest named monitor because his Nasdaq council recusal controls are unverified, not because an adverse record was found. No open §24 Filter 1 integrity signal, and no person trips a gate. [People Integrity Dossiers, AKAM, §§1–2, 2026-09-14]
- **Checklist read:** 64.8% answered; nine Red items — A4-02 audit-firm calibre, A4-13 firm/partner discipline, A6-04 equity burn/overhang, A6-05 conditional single-trigger vesting, A6-09 problematic pay practice, A7a-01 lease-related commitments, A8-05 recurring restructuring, A10-03 stock-plan dissent, and A14-01 leverage. RPT & leakage risk at 45 is the binding Checklist Risk because the aggregate RPT ledger and group cash topology are missing.
- The biggest governance flag is RF-CAP-004, High: repeated acquisitions without return proof; it caps capital allocation at 50 and floors Governance risk at 60.
- Business-model/01 hard-disqualifier result, verbatim: **“No disqualifier triggered.”**
- §24 rejector filters: serial acquirer tripped, applying CapAlloc max 50 and GovRisk floor 60; turnaround, structurally unaligned owner, and unresolved-integrity filters did not trip.
- Partial-data caps apply to incentive alignment, usefulness, shareholder friendliness, board reliance, audit assurance, RPT risk, people/network integrity, legal risk, and confidence. The legal floor moved LegalRisk from 32 to 40; other capped scores were already inside their limits.
- The single highest-value next data request is the filed FY2026 DEF 14A in the frozen evidence set.
- **This module supersedes business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance. Treat this module’s governance verdict and scores as the primary governance read.**
- Preserve RF-DISC-002 in the cross-module forensic roll-up; recurring SBC/restructuring and the large GAAP-to-adjusted gap may compound with earnings-quality or balance-sheet flags.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management met the one completed quarterly guide, but one quarter is not a long record. [Q1 FY2026 earnings release, Financial Guidance, p.2; Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Results, p.1]
- Capital has not proven per-share value creation; acquisitions lack return scorecards and buybacks exceeded strict FCF. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.7]
- The available pay evidence mainly rewards service and retention, with high burn and conditional single-trigger vesting. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18, pp.84–86 and Exhibit 10.5, §6(c)–(e)]
- The founder-CEO owns 1.9%; observed sales were plan-driven, no observed open-market buy was found, and the full record is missing. [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified public SEC filing supplement; Web: SEC Forms 4, 2025-11-07 to 2026-07-15 — unverified public filing sweep]
- An independent chair, 8/9 independent directors, and one-share/one-vote help minority holders, but stock-plan dissent and takeover limits weaken the read. [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp.27–29 and Appendix C-1, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified public SEC filing supplement; Web: SEC Form 8-K, Item 5.07, 2026-05-13 — unverified public filing supplement]
- Management discloses the GAAP bridge, but recurring exclusions make adjusted performance look much better. [Q2 FY2026 Supplemental Financial Information, GAAP-to-Non-GAAP Reconciliation sheet]
- No hard disqualifier or Critical red flag was confirmed; six High flags remain. [Business-Model Disqualifier Scan, Verdict-Lock Signal; Management-Governance Red-Flag Register, 2026-09-14]
- The module is useful but capped: current filings support the core negative capital-allocation call, while the missing proxy and incomplete legal/network work limit confidence.

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Partial
- **Reason:** The frozen pool has current audited and interim filings, transcripts, an audit report, commitments disclosure, and three-year capital-allocation history, but it omits the proxy/compensation disclosure, full ownership and insider-transaction data, board and voting disclosure, related-party detail, and auditor-fee disclosure required for a complete governance read.
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record; capital allocation; candor; people dossiers (from the seed register, subject to the discovery loop); audit quality (except fee independence); RPT/group forensics (limited); contingent liabilities; accounting forensics; regulatory/legal (subject to its independent database sweep). Incentives, ownership, and board/shareholder-rights agents run only to record their data-limited conclusions and caps.
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — no verdict lock was triggered. That scan separately preserves the ownership/pledging and RPT gaps as not assessable. [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md, Verdict-Lock Signal]
- **Active partial-data caps:**
  - No proxy / compensation disclosure: incentive alignment maximum 50; overall usefulness maximum 70.
  - No ownership / comprehensive insider-transaction data: shareholder friendliness maximum 60.
  - Board independence, committee, election, and rights read: not assessable from the signature roster.
  - No related-party note: RPT quantification not assessable; Amber disclosure finding.
  - No auditor-fee disclosure: audit & assurance quality maximum 65; confidence maximum 80.
- **Critical missing items:**
  - FY2026 DEF 14A, including CD&A, named-executive pay, beneficial ownership, board committees/independence, related-party transactions, auditor fees, voting and shareholder-rights disclosures.
  - Form 4 / authoritative insider-transaction history and Schedule 13D/13G or equivalent ownership data.
- **Single highest-value missing document:** FY2026 DEF 14A proxy statement.

# Management & Track Record — AKAM

| COO and GM, Cloud Technology Group | Adam Karon | March 2021 | 5.5 years | Joined Akamai in 2005; formerly led the Media and Carrier Division and Global Services and Support. | [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, executive-officer biography, p. 25, filed 2026-03-05 — unverified, used only for background and tenure] |

Leighton is a co-founder, so the CEO is founder-led. This report does not assess ownership alignment or incentives; those belong to the ownership and compensation specialists.

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | **Not assessable from the frozen pool.** It lacks the FY2026 proxy and a complete 2023–26 Form 8-K / officer-change history. The available FY2025 10-K and Q2 2026 call show the same CEO and CFO, but cannot establish that no other senior departures occurred. | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 10, p. 95; Q2 2026 earnings call, participants, p. 3] |
| CFO turnover | McGowan’s disclosed March 2019 appointment and continued CFO role in the Q2 2026 call support continuity for the current CFO; a complete three-year turnover audit is not available. No sudden-CFO-resignation signal is evidenced in the admitted documents. | [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, p. 25, filed 2026-03-05 — unverified, used only for tenure; Q2 2026 earnings call, participants, p. 3] |
| CEO succession / continuity | Leighton has served as CEO since January 2013. A formal succession plan is not assessable because the proxy-equivalent disclosure is absent from the frozen pool. | [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 22, 24 — unverified, used only for tenure; FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 10, p. 95] |

The five senior operating roles above have a median 5.5 years in their present roles. That clears the A13-04 four-year benchmark, but the missing turnover and succession disclosures limit confidence.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Management’s stated strategy is to use Akamai’s distributed platform to provide security, compute, delivery and AI-infrastructure services, with investment focused on higher-growth security and Cloud Infrastructure Services (CIS). [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1, pp. 3–5; Item 7, p. 29] In Q2, management said that CIS commitments signed so far in 2026 exceeded $2.8 billion and anticipated total-company revenue growth accelerating into the low teens in 2027; this is a forward target, not delivered revenue. [Q2 2026 earnings call, CEO prepared remarks, p. 4] Its published FY2026 targets are revenue of $4.445–$4.530 billion, CIS constant-currency growth of at least 50%, Security growth in the high single digits, a mid-single-digit decline in Delivery and other cloud applications, and non-GAAP operating margin of 25%–26%. [Q2 2026 earnings call, CFO prepared remarks, pp. 7–8]

## 4. Promise vs Delivery

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q2 2026 revenue of $1.075–$1.100bn | Q1 2026, 2026-05-07 | Q2 revenue was $1.099682bn, $0.318m below the high end and within the range. | Kept | [Q1 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2] vs [Q2 2026 earnings release, Results, p. 1] |
| Q2 2026 non-GAAP EPS of $1.45–$1.65 and non-GAAP operating margin of 25%–26% | Q1 2026, 2026-05-07 | Q2 non-GAAP EPS was $1.59 and non-GAAP operating margin was 25%, both within guidance, with margin at the low end. | Kept, at the lower margin bound | [Q1 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2] vs [Q2 2026 earnings release, Results, p. 1] |
| FY2026 revenue $4.445–$4.550bn, non-GAAP operating margin about 26%, and non-GAAP EPS $6.40–$7.15 | Q1 2026, 2026-05-07 | In Q2 the lower revenue and EPS ends were retained, but the revenue high end fell $20m to $4.530bn, margin became 25%–26%, and EPS high end fell $0.10 to $7.05. No FY2026 outcome is available as of 2026-09-14. | Narrowed; open | [Q1 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2] vs [Q2 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2] |
| Overall revenue growth accelerating into the low teens in 2027, supported by signed CIS commitments | Q2 2026, 2026-08-06 | Not yet testable. Management said the new $600m commitment had no material FY2026 revenue impact and would ramp through 2027. | Open — not assessable | [Q2 2026 earnings call, CEO and CFO prepared remarks, pp. 4, 6] |

The pool permits only one completed management guidance interval. The cross-module earnings read separately finds four consecutive final-Street revenue and normalized-EPS beats through Q2 2026, but those are not a substitute for a longer management-guidance record; the same source-bound annual check records a FY2025 GAAP EPS miss of 9%. [Earnings/04 Guidance & Consensus, §§6–7; CIQ Estimates→Surprise, annual, source-bound facts sidecar]

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | No. The current cloud build-out is a strategy change under a long-serving CEO and CFO, not a new-management turnaround. It should be judged on delivered CIS revenue and returns, not the plan. | [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 24–26 — unverified, used only for tenure; Q2 2026 earnings call, pp. 4, 6–8] |
| If yes: years of *proven* operating inflection actually delivered (not promised) | Not applicable. No turnaround credit or RF-MGT-004 penalty is applied. | [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 24–26 — unverified, used only for tenure] |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? (not evidence) | No. The management read gives no credit for the résumé or the low-teens 2027 plan; it credits only the Q2 guide outcome. | [Q1 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2] vs [Q2 2026 earnings release, Results, p. 1] |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | No unverified adverse integrity signal was routed in the available handoff. This is not an independent person-integrity clearance; agent 07 owns that work. RF-MGT-005 is not emitted. | [Business-model/01 Disqualifier Scan, §§2–3, 2026-09-14] |

## 5. Management Read

This is a stable, founder-led management group: the CEO has served 13.7 years and the CFO 7.5 years, while the five-role operating group has a 5.5-year median role tenure. [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 24–26 — unverified, used only for background and tenure] The only completed current-year guide was kept, but Q2 reduced FY2026’s revenue and EPS high ends and extended the non-GAAP margin range down to 25%; this is a limited, mixed execution record rather than proof of a long record of promises kept. [Q1 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2] vs [Q2 2026 earnings release, Results and Financial guidance, pp. 1–2]

The biggest management signal is that the CIS-led plan is measurable but still unproven: signed commitments above $2.8bn and a low-teens 2027 growth target do not establish revenue conversion or return on the extra capital. [Q2 2026 earnings call, CEO and CFO prepared remarks, pp. 4, 6–8]

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — AKAM

AKAM reports in USD under U.S. GAAP. This is a FY2023–FY2025 scorecard, with H1 2026 used only to test the latest direction. FCF means cash from operations (CFO) less total cash capex, including capitalized internal-use software.

## 1. Uses of Capital (3–5 years)

| Use | Cumulative Amount | Currency | % of CFO (or FCF) | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Acquisitions (cash) | 595.4 | USDm | 13.6% of $4,386.4m CFO | [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies Inc NasdaqGS AKAM Financials.xls, Cash Flow sheet, FY2023–FY2025] |
| Buybacks | 2,374.6 | USDm | 54.1% of CFO; 110.4% of $2,151.6m FCF | [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies Inc NasdaqGS AKAM Financials.xls, Cash Flow sheet, FY2023–FY2025; data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, Statements of Cash Flows, p.55] |
| Dividends | 0.0 | USDm | 0.0% of CFO | [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies Inc NasdaqGS AKAM Financials.xls, Cash Flow sheet, FY2023–FY2025] |
| Capex | 2,234.8 | USDm | 51.0% of CFO | [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, Statements of Cash Flows, p.55] |
| Debt repayment | 1,490.0 | USDm | 34.0% of CFO | [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies Inc NasdaqGS AKAM Financials.xls, Cash Flow sheet, FY2023–FY2025] |

The amounts are cash-flow categories, so they are not additive measures of CFO: debt repayment can be funded with refinancing or balance-sheet cash. The three-year FCF build is $4,386.4m CFO less $2,234.8m total capex = $2,151.6m. Buybacks therefore exceeded internally generated FCF by $223.0m before acquisitions and debt repayment. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, Statements of Cash Flows, p.55]

## 2. M&A Scorecard

| Deal | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost (divestitures forced / focus lost / options foregone) | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** Cash acquisitions were $598.8m, $872.1m, $106.2m, $434.1m, and $55.1m in FY2021–FY2025: $2,066.3m in total. Three annual amounts were more than $400m, and the programme continued with the $205.0m LayerX acquisition in July 2026. This is a serial-acquirer pattern for score-cap purposes, even though the available evidence does not show debt-funded acquisitions near or above AKAM’s own value. No deal-level return, synergy delivery, or comparison with the organic cloud build is disclosed; do not infer success from the acquisition rationale. **RF-CAP-004 is triggered (High); the capital-allocation score is capped at 50, although the raw score below is already lower.** [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies Inc NasdaqGS AKAM Financials.xls, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025; data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., Q2 2026.pdf, Form 10-Q, Note 6 (Acquisition)]

The H1 2026 share-count result is a positive fact: buybacks were not merely a stock-based-compensation dilution offset. It does not establish price discipline. The current price is a later market observation, and this module has no fair-value work, historical repurchase-price range, or disclosed annual repurchase share count to decide whether the purchases created value.

As a directional, non-equivalent read, CIQ reported return on capital fell from 5.3% in FY2023 to 4.3% in FY2024, 3.9% in FY2025, and 2.7% LTM Jun. 2026. | [ciq_facts.json, multi_year_trajectory, 2026-09-14 — CIQ vendor basis] |

ROIC is the profit earned for each $100 invested. The CIQ return-on-capital series is not the required incremental-ROIC formula, so it is not substituted for it. Its decline nevertheless matters when total capex rose to $819.5m in FY2025 and the company recorded FY2023–FY2025 restructuring actions of $135.326m, including product retirements and impairments of capitalized software and acquired intangibles; not all of those charges are impairments or attributable to a particular deal. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, Statements of Cash Flows, p.55; Note 10 (Restructuring), pp.74–75]

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

**Mixed.** The best proven use is the H1 2026 buyback result: 4.998m shares repurchased exceeded 3.879m issuances/reissues and reduced shares outstanding by 1.120m, so the programme was not just dilution offset. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., Q2 2026.pdf, Form 10-Q, Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12]

The worst use is the repeated acquisition programme: $2.066bn of FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions and $205.0m for LayerX have no disclosed return, synergy, or alternative-use comparison in the admitted evidence, which triggers the serial-acquirer cap rather than an assumption of value creation. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies Inc NasdaqGS AKAM Financials.xls, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025; data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., Q2 2026.pdf, Form 10-Q, Note 6 (Acquisition)]

The single biggest signal is the May 2026 $3.500bn convertible issuance for accelerated cloud infrastructure: filed convertible principal reached $7.640bn at June 30 while H1 strict FCF was only $221.174m and buybacks were $615.744m. The contrary fact is that the notes carry zero coupons and CIQ reports 32.6x LTM EBITDA/interest coverage, so this is not an immediate cash-interest distress call; it is a return-on-investment and balance-sheet-capacity concern. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., Q2 2026.pdf, Form 10-Q, Note 7, pp.17–20; Statements of Cash Flows, p.7; ciq_facts.json, interest_coverage_x, 2026-09-14 — CIQ vendor basis]

Per-share value creation is therefore not proven: H1 share count improved, but FY2023–FY2025 GAAP diluted EPS fell from $3.52 to $3.07 and the vendor return-on-capital series also fell. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, Statements of Operations, p.54; ciq_facts.json, multi_year_trajectory, 2026-09-14 — CIQ vendor basis]

# Incentives & Compensation — AKAM

**Partial-data limit:** The FY2026 definitive proxy, which contains the CEO/CFO compensation tables, annual-bonus and long-term-incentive (LTIP) targets and weights, pay ratio, director-pay table, and say-on-pay result, is not in the frozen evidence set. FY2025 Form 10-K incorporates that proxy by reference rather than reproducing it. Incentive alignment is therefore **not assessable at executive level** and is capped at 50/100. [FY2025 Form 10-K, cover — Documents Incorporated by Reference; Item 11—Executive Compensation, p.95]

## 1. Compensation Structure

| Executive | Base | Annual Bonus | Long-Term Incentive | Cash vs Equity Mix | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| F. Thomson Leighton, CEO | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | Not disclosed for the CEO; company-wide plan includes service-, TSR- and financial-performance RSUs/restricted stock | Not assessable | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 11—Executive Compensation, p.95] |
| Edward J. McGowan, CFO | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | Not disclosed for the CFO; company-wide plan includes service-, TSR- and financial-performance RSUs/restricted stock | Not assessable | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 11—Executive Compensation, p.95] |

Reporting currency is USD and the filing basis is U.S. GAAP; the latest annual period is FY2025 ended 2025-12-31. The missing proxy is material: company-wide stock-plan disclosures do not show the CEO/CFO pay mix or what each executive can earn.

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Annual bonus | CEO/CFO annual-bonus metrics and weights are not disclosed in the frozen pool | Not disclosed | Not assessable | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 11—Executive Compensation, p.95] |
| Company-wide FY2025 service awards | Continued service; 6.499m of 6.883m FY2025 RSU/restricted-stock/DSU grants (94.42% by units) | 94.42% of company-wide FY2025 grant units; not an executive-pay weight | Neither: retention/time-based, not a per-share value measure | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18—Stock-Based Compensation, pp.85–86] |
| Company-wide FY2025 market-based awards | Relative total shareholder return (TSR) against a peer group; 0.249m grant units | 3.62% of company-wide FY2025 grant units; not an executive-pay weight | Returns-based | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18—Stock-Based Compensation, pp.85–86] |
| Company-wide FY2025 performance-based awards | Defined internal financial metrics, not named in the filing; 0.135m grant units | 1.96% of company-wide FY2025 grant units; not an executive-pay weight | Not assessable: the filing does not identify whether the metrics are per-share/returns or size-based | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18—Stock-Based Compensation, pp.85–86] |

The disclosed mix is a grant-unit mix across employees, officers, directors, consultants and advisers, not the CEO/CFO LTIP mix. It establishes that TSR and financial-performance awards exist, but cannot establish the actual executive scorecard or whether it rewards revenue, absolute EBITDA, EPS, ROIC, free cash flow per share, or something else.

| Company-wide equity cost, not CEO/CFO pay | FY2025 stock-based compensation was $459.402m, equal to 101.63% of $452.031m GAAP net income. This is not a measure of named-executive pay. | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18—Stock-Based Compensation, p.84; Earnings/06 Earnings Quality, §4] |

## 3A. Statutory & Code Pay Gates (A6-01, A6-02, A6-05, A6-06, A6-09)

| Test | Raw value | Band applied | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Aggregate managerial pay vs the statutory cap (India: Sec 197 — 11% aggregate; ≤5% single MD/WTD, ≤10% all executives, NEDs ≤1%/3%; loss years per Schedule V) | Not disclosed; India statutory test does not apply to this U.S. issuer | U.S. local disclosure is the proxy; compensation amounts absent | Insufficient Data | [FY2025 Form 10-K, cover; Item 11, p.95] |
| Promoter-executive pay vs the extra gates (India: LODR Reg 17(6)(e) — ₹5cr or 2.5% of net profit individually, 5% aggregate) | Not applicable to a U.S. Nasdaq issuer; generic CEO/CFO peer comparison is also unavailable | India promoter gates not applicable; peer-pay evidence absent | Insufficient Data | [FY2025 Form 10-K, cover; Item 11, p.95] |
| MD/CEO pay growth vs 3-year PAT CAGR | Not disclosed | Pay and annual executive-pay history absent | Insufficient Data | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 11, p.95] |
| CEO-to-median ratio + trend of the MEDIAN (rising exec pay on a falling median is the flag) | Not disclosed | Ratio, methodology, and median-pay trend absent | Insufficient Data | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 11, p.95] |
| Severance / change-of-control terms (double-trigger only; ≤2 years' pay) | Cash severance not disclosed. The Executive RSU form conditionally provides full vesting at a change in control if the award is not exchanged for a replacement award; if it is replaced, vesting accelerates upon a qualifying termination within 12 months. | Conditional single-trigger equity acceleration breaches the stated double-trigger-only standard; cash multiple cannot be tested | Red | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Exhibit 10.5—Form of Global Executive RSU Agreement, §6(c)–(e)] |
| Clawback / malus policy (and: was any trigger acted on?) | Executive RSU form subjects awards/proceeds to company clawback or recoupment policies and misconduct/fraud/law-based recoupment. FY2025 cover reports no error correction requiring a Rule 10D-1 recovery analysis. Lookback, covered executives, and actual recoveries are not disclosed. | Policy existence is positive; full Rule 10D-1 / three-year test cannot be completed | Amber | [FY2025 Form 10-K, cover—Rule 10D-1 check box; Exhibit 10.5—Form of Global Executive RSU Agreement, §10(g)] |
| Problematic practices (repricing without a vote, gross-ups, single-trigger vesting, guaranteed multi-year bonuses) | Conditional single-trigger vesting if a change-in-control award is not replaced; repricing, gross-up, and guaranteed-bonus terms are not disclosed. | Any single-trigger change-of-control vesting is a Red-band practice | Red | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Exhibit 10.5—Form of Global Executive RSU Agreement, §6(c)–(e)] |
| Dissent response (any ≥20% vote against pay met with a disclosed response ≤6 months?) | Say-on-pay vote and board response not disclosed | Support and response unavailable | Insufficient Data | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 11, p.95] |

## 3B. Human Capital (A12-01 … A12-04)

| Test | Raw value | Band applied | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Attrition vs industry median | Management says voluntary attrition was “slightly up” in 2025 versus 2024; no percentage or industry median given | Not stable, but neither >1.5× industry nor a spike is evidenced | Amber | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1—Human Capital, pp.5–6] |
| Median-employee pay level & change vs executive change | Not disclosed; management reports annual internal pay-equity analyses and no widespread disparity, but not median pay or its change | Median-versus-executive test cannot be calculated | Insufficient Data | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1—Human Capital, pp.5–6; Item 11, p.95] |
| ESOP pool breadth (broad-based vs top-concentrated); promoters excluded? | Stock awards held by the “vast majority” of employees; most employees eligible for ESPP. FY2025 ESPP issued 0.966m shares. | Broad-based retention-tool evidence; executive concentration is not separately quantified | Green | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1—Human Capital, pp.5–6; Note 18—Stock-Based Compensation, pp.84–85] |
| Workplace-conduct record (POSH / labor / safety, disclosed + resolved) | Company states it does not tolerate discrimination and requires annual ethics/compliance and data-security training, but gives no complaint, labor, or safety case statistics or resolution data | Policy disclosure alone does not meet the clean/resolved-record test | Insufficient Data | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1—Human Capital, pp.5–6] |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

**Mixed.** The disclosed equity plan contains TSR and financial-performance awards, but the filing gives neither the CEO/CFO cash/equity mix nor their actual performance metrics and weights; executive pay cannot therefore be judged as per-share-value or size-led. The strongest disclosed negative is conditional single-trigger change-of-control equity vesting, alongside FY2025 award burn of 4.73% of FY2025 weighted-average basic shares—above the 3% Red band—although these grants are company-wide rather than CEO/CFO-only. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18, pp.84–86; Exhibit 10.5, §6(c)–(e)]

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — AKAM

The frozen evidence set does not include the 2026 proxy or a Capital IQ ownership export. The FY2025 Form 10-K incorporates its ownership disclosure by reference to that proxy, while the CIQ facts sidecar marks insider activity, open-market activity, top holders and institutional-holder trend as missing. The SEC filings cited as web sources below are useful context, but are not frozen-pool evidence and are labelled unverified. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 12, p.95; CIQ facts sidecar, ownership fields, frozen 2026-09-14 — vendor data]

## 1. Ownership

| Holder | % Held | Share Class | Bought or Granted? | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| F. Thomson Leighton, co-founder and CEO | 1.9% (2,759,456 shares) | Common Stock | The source identifies 2,529,963 trust-held shares and 108,358 foundation shares; it also includes 72,000 RSUs vesting within 60 days. It does not establish the cash cost or acquisition history of the trust-held stake. | [Web: SEC EDGAR 2026 preliminary proxy, Security Ownership, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-05 (unverified)] |
| Other executive officers and directors, aggregate | ~0.4% (557,750 shares, derived as 3,317,206 less Leighton’s 2,759,456) | Common Stock | Mixed and not fully separable from grants: the group total includes 220,092 shares issuable within 60 days. Cash-purchase history is not disclosed in the available source. | [Web: SEC EDGAR 2026 preliminary proxy, Security Ownership, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-05 (unverified)] |
| The Vanguard Group | 12.7% (18,376,759 shares) | Common Stock | Not applicable — institutional holder. Proxy basis is Vanguard’s 2025 Schedule 13G/A. | [Web: SEC EDGAR 2026 preliminary proxy, Security Ownership, p.27, filed 2026-03-05 (unverified)] |
| BlackRock, Inc. | 6.9% (10,026,738 shares) | Common Stock | Not applicable — institutional holder. Proxy basis is BlackRock’s 2026 Schedule 13G/A. | [Web: SEC EDGAR 2026 preliminary proxy, Security Ownership, p.27, filed 2026-03-05 (unverified)] |
| Promoter / controlling bloc | None identified | Common Stock | Not applicable — Akamai is a U.S. issuer with no disclosed promoter group or holder close to voting control in the available ownership table. | [Web: SEC EDGAR 2026 preliminary proxy, Security Ownership, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-05 (unverified)] |

Total insider ownership is 2.3% (3,317,206 shares across 18 executive officers and directors) as of February 20, 2026. This SEC-web figure includes shares acquirable within 60 days, so it is not a current economic-interest or cash-purchase measure. [Web: SEC EDGAR 2026 preliminary proxy, Security Ownership, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-05 (unverified)]

| Robert Blumofe, CTO | Sell | 3,000 common shares at weighted-average $87.6836 | 2025-11-14 | Sale under the same Rule 10b5-1 plan. | [Web: SEC Form 4, 2025-11-14, pp.1–2 (unverified)] |
| Paul Joseph, EVP Global Sales | Sell | 5,000 common shares at weighted-average $87.6902 | 2025-11-17 | Sale under a Rule 10b5-1 plan adopted 2025-03-17. | [Web: SEC Form 4, 2025-11-17, pp.1–2 (unverified)] |
| Paul Joseph, EVP Global Sales | Sell | 5,000 common shares at weighted-average $90.1749 | 2026-01-15 | Sale under the 2025-03-17 Rule 10b5-1 plan. | [Web: SEC Form 4, 2026-01-15, pp.1–2 (unverified)] |
| Kim Salem-Jackson, CMO | Grant / tax withholding | 5,911 shares issued at $0; 2,791 shares withheld at $94.17 | 2026-02-20 | The acquired shares paid the 2025 bonus award; the disposition was tax withholding, not an open-market sale. | [Web: SEC Form 4, 2026-02-20, pp.1–2 (unverified)] |
| Aaron Ahola, General Counsel | PRSU vest / tax withholding | 8,731 shares vested; 2,497 shares withheld at $109.31 | 2026-02-19 | Performance RSU settlement after 2025 results certification; no open-market purchase. | [Web: SEC Form 4, 2026-02-19, pp.1–2 (unverified)] |

Net insider activity: **not determinable for the full 12 months from frozen evidence.** The SEC-web sample identifies 19,600 shares of open-market sales (about $2.15m using the reported sale prices) and no open-market purchase; all five identified sales were under pre-set Rule 10b5-1 plans. That is observed net selling, not a complete or discretionary-insider-conviction measure. The frozen Q1 and Q2 filings separately record plan actions: Joseph, Salem-Jackson and Blumofe adopted plans in March, and Sundaram adopted a sale plan for up to 7,022 shares in May. [Web: SEC Forms 4, 2025-11-07, 2025-11-14, 2025-11-17, 2026-01-15 and 2026-07-15 (unverified); Q1 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Item 5, p.59; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Item 5, p.62]

## 5. Ownership / Insider Read

Founder-CEO ownership is meaningful as personal exposure but not control: Leighton held 1.9% and the entire executive-officer/director group 2.3% at the disclosed February date. The trust-held founder stake is distinct from the small near-term RSU component, but its original cash cost is not proven. [Web: SEC EDGAR 2026 preliminary proxy, Security Ownership, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-05 (unverified)]

The observed $2.15m of sales were all under Rule 10b5-1 plans, while vesting and tax-withholding entries are not discretionary buys or sales. The main minority-holder risk from this lens is evidence quality: the missing frozen proxy/ownership export means actual pledges, complete net insider activity, institutional trends and any shareholder bloc cannot be assessed; they must not be assumed benign. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 12, p.95; CIQ facts sidecar, ownership fields, frozen 2026-09-14 — vendor data]

# Board & Shareholder Rights — AKAM

## 1. Board Composition

**Verdict: Board independent; shareholder rights adequate but constrained.** The latest detailed board disclosure is the filed 2026 DEF 14A, which was not included in the frozen evidence generation. It is used below only as a clearly labelled public SEC-filing supplement; it does not remove the frozen-pool gap. The frozen FY2025 Form 10-K incorporates the proxy into Items 10–14, confirming that the missing proxy is where the board, ownership, related-party and shareholder-rights disclosures belong. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, Items 10–14, filed 2026-02-20]

At the May 13, 2026 annual meeting, shareholders elected nine directors. Eight of the nine were classified independent by the company under Nasdaq rules; the exception was co-founder and CEO F. Thomson Leighton. The Board chair, Dan Hesse, is independent and separate from the CEO. This is a favourable structural fact, not proof that every director is free of all commercial influence. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, filed 2026-03-31, pp. 10–11 and 27–28 — public filing not in frozen pool; SEC 8-K, filed 2026-05-13, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool]

| Feature | Current read | Evidence and qualification | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Board size and independence | Nine directors elected; eight classified independent (88.9%). | Board size is the post-meeting elected slate. Independence classification is the proxy’s, not an independent legal conclusion. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 10–11 and 27–28 — public filing not in frozen pool; SEC 8-K, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool] | Green |
| Chair / CEO separation | Dan Hesse is independent chair; Leighton is CEO and director. | Hesse leads independent-director sessions, board evaluation and CEO-performance oversight according to the proxy. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 27–28 — public filing not in frozen pool] | Green |
| Director-linked commercial connections | The proxy reports 2025 sales of $36.4m and purchases of $1.4m involving Bas Burger’s then-employer BT International and affiliates; it also reports $1.3m of sales and $0.5m of purchases involving Madhu Ranganathan’s then-employer OpenText. The company states each was below 1% of the relevant company’s revenue and on normal commercial terms. | These are independence-context disclosures, not a conclusion that the flows are Item 404 related-party transactions. Burger was BT International CEO from April 2025 to February 2026; Ranganathan was an OpenText executive through March 2025. The $37.8m BT gross flow merits monitoring even though the company classified Burger as independent. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 28–29 — public filing not in frozen pool] | Amber |
| Board skills evidence | A skills matrix is disclosed, but the underlying skill-by-director entries were not transcribed in the frozen evidence and were not independently rebuilt from the public filing. | A claimed five-competency mapping would exceed the evidence reviewed. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 12–13 — public filing not in frozen pool; Governance Data Triage, AKAM, 2026-09-14] | Insufficient Data |

The sources do not document dissent, private auditor sessions, or the quality of challenge at meetings. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 30–32 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Annual evaluation | The Board reports annual evaluation through surveys, interviews and discussion led by the independent chair and ESG Committee chair, with an external consultant involved. | Methodology is disclosed, but discrete actions or outcomes were not described. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 27–28 — public filing not in frozen pool] |

Directors Ford and Verwaayen, each appointed in 2013, were not nominated for re-election in 2026. | This supports renewal. The sources do not provide a complete three-year departure log or reasons for every departure. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 10–11 and 26–27 — public filing not in frozen pool] |

## 2. Tenure & Refreshment

Using the disclosed initial appointment years for the nine elected directors and calendar 2026, the median tenure is approximately seven years (calculated: 1, 1, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 28 years). This is below the module’s nine-year independent-director median reference point. The conclusion carries two qualifications: Leighton’s 28-year tenure is as founder/CEO rather than independent director, and the calculation is an analyst calculation from disclosed appointment years, not a company-reported statistic. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 19–26 — public filing not in frozen pool]

Four of nine 2026 nominees are women and three are racially or ethnically diverse under the proxy’s stated definitions. The Board also states that it considers diversity in nominations. This supports diversity disclosure, but not a conclusion on representation of every stakeholder group. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 12–13 — public filing not in frozen pool]

| Test | Result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Median tenure / renewal | Approx. seven-year median across the elected nine; seven new directors and eight transitions since 2017. | Green |
| Long-tenure concentration | CEO Leighton has served since 1998; independent directors Hesse and Miller were appointed in 2016 and 2015, respectively. Long service is visible, but no long-tenured independent bloc is shown. | Amber |
| Annual election / accountability | Nine directors were elected at the 2026 annual meeting; the proxy describes annual elections and a majority-voting policy for uncontested director elections. | Green |
| Resignation / transition transparency | Planned 2026 Ford and Verwaayen transitions are disclosed. A complete exit-reason test is not available. | Amber |

## 3. Related-Party Transactions & Group Leakage

The FY2025 Form 10-K incorporates related-party disclosure from the absent proxy, so the frozen evidence alone cannot support a complete related-party transaction (RPT) conclusion. The public 2026 DEF 14A states that no transaction requiring Item 404(a) disclosure occurred in 2025 and describes a review process for possible transactions involving directors, officers and their immediate family. This is a favourable legal-disclosure statement, not proof of zero RPTs under a broader economic definition. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 13, p. 95; SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, filed 2026-03-31, p. 39 — public filing not in frozen pool]

| Potential relationship / control | Amount / terms disclosed | Approval / disclosure read | Assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| Item 404(a) reportable transactions in 2025 | The proxy reports none. | The proxy says transactions involving a director or the General Counsel are reviewed by disinterested Audit Committee members; other executive-officer transactions are reviewed by the General Counsel after notice to the Audit Committee and Board chairs. | Favourable but scope-limited. It does not prove the absence of all transactions with all affiliates. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, p. 39 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Burger / BT International commercial relationship | $36.4m sales; $1.4m purchases in 2025. | Proxy says the transactions were in the ordinary course, on normal commercial terms and each less than 1% of the applicable party’s revenue. | Independence-context relationship; do not classify as an Item 404 RPT without the filing doing so. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 28–29 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Ranganathan / OpenText commercial relationship | $1.3m sales; $0.5m purchases in 2025. | Same proxy qualification: ordinary course, normal commercial terms and below 1% of applicable revenue. | Independence-context relationship; do not classify as an Item 404 RPT without the filing doing so. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 28–29 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Vendor relationship graph | Zero sources, counterparties and relationship rows. | The graph has no scope notes or rows, so it cannot validate either absence or concentration. | Insufficient data; zero rows are not zero relationships. [relationships.json, AKAM, frozen 2026-09-14 — Tier-5 vendor export] |

| Ratio check | Numerator basis | Denominator basis | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| RPT exposure / revenue | No matched aggregate RPT numerator disclosed. An Item 404(a) “none” statement is a legal disclosure result, not a value numerator. | No same-scope RPT denominator is available. | **Not assessable.** A clean percentage would mix a legal disclosure threshold with an economic exposure measure. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, p. 39 — public filing not in frozen pool; Governance Data Triage, AKAM, 2026-09-14] |

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

AKAM has one disclosed Nasdaq-listed common-stock class and the proxy states one vote per common share. That supports a simple common-share voting structure. Its charter and by-laws nevertheless contain standard U.S. takeover defences: the Board can issue undesignated preferred stock, fill vacancies, impose advance-notice rules for nominations and proposals, and Delaware’s business-combination statute can restrict certain transactions with 15% holders for three years without Board approval. These provisions can make a change of control harder even if an offer could benefit shareholders. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, cover page and pp. 24–25; SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 108–110 — public filing not in frozen pool]

| Right / defense | Current position | Minority-holder implication |
|---|---|---|
| Common-share voting | One vote per common share; no unequal common-share voting class disclosed. | Positive. The conclusion is limited to common stock; the charter’s undesignated preferred-stock authority remains. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 108–110 — public filing not in frozen pool; FY2025 Form 10-K, pp. 24–25] |
| Director elections | Annual elections; majority voting in uncontested elections and a resignation policy for a director who does not receive more “for” than “against” votes. | Positive accountability mechanism. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 108–110 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Proxy access | A shareholder or group may nominate up to 20 nominees or 25% of the Board (whichever is greater) after holding at least 3% for three years. | Usable but demanding for a small holder. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 108–110 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Special meetings | Following shareholder approval at the May 2026 annual meeting, holders of at least 25% may demand a special meeting, subject to ownership and continuing-hold requirements. | A meaningful right, but the 25% threshold is high for dispersed holders. [SEC 8-K, filed 2026-05-13, Items 5.03 and 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Written consent | Charter / by-laws require shareholder action at a duly called meeting rather than written consent. | Restrictive. [SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 108–110 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Anti-takeover provisions | Advance notice, Board vacancy-filling, blank-check preferred-stock authority, and Delaware Section 203 are disclosed. | Restrictive; no inference is made about how the Board would use them in a future offer. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, pp. 24–25] |

## 4A. Shareholder Voting & Dilution

The key voting signal is the approval of an amendment to the 2013 Stock Incentive Plan that added 8.0m shares, with 66,180,915 votes for and 47,672,263 against. Excluding abstentions, the against share was **41.9%**: 47,672,263 / (66,180,915 + 47,672,263). This is far above the module’s 20% high-opposition trigger. The outcome is a shareholder-approved plan amendment, not evidence that the plan is improper; the 8-K does not state the Board’s response to the scale of opposition. [SEC 8-K, filed 2026-05-13, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool]

| 2026 annual-meeting vote | For | Against | Against share, excluding abstentions | Read |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Election of Janaki Akella | 104,263,508 | 9,580,136 | 8.4% | Largest director-election opposition; below the 20% red threshold. [SEC 8-K, 2026-05-13, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| 2013 Stock Incentive Plan amendment (+8.0m shares) | 66,180,915 | 47,672,263 | 41.9% | **Red voting-pattern signal.** [SEC 8-K, 2026-05-13, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool; SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, pp. 70–79 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Advisory say-on-pay | 102,400,844 | 10,535,188 | 9.3% | Passed; opposition below 20%. [SEC 8-K, 2026-05-13, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Special-meeting right | 113,217,654 | 628,777 | 0.6% | Strong approval of the new 25% right. [SEC 8-K, 2026-05-13, Items 5.03 and 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool] |
| Shareholder political-spending-report proposal | 51,672,536 | 61,327,064 | 54.3% against | The shareholder proposal was not approved, but the 45.7% support is a material minority signal. No response was identified in the reviewed sources. [SEC 8-K, 2026-05-13, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool] |

## 4B. Corporate-Actions Fairness

No group merger, delisting, preferential allotment, rights issue or qualified-institutional placement was identified in the FY2025 Form 10-K or Q1/Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q disclosures reviewed. This is a statement about the reviewed filings, not a claim that no corporate action can occur. The 8.0m-share stock-plan amendment is not a preferential allotment, but it is a dilution-related action and had material opposition. [data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., 2025.pdf, FY2025 Form 10-K, filed 2026-02-20; data/AKAM/Akamai Technologies, Inc., Q1 2026.pdf and Q2 2026.pdf, Forms 10-Q; SEC 8-K, filed 2026-05-13, Item 5.07 — public filing not in frozen pool]

| Action | Fairness read | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Group merger / affiliate buyout | No such action identified in the filings reviewed; no transaction fairness analysis is therefore available. | Not Applicable |
| Stock-plan share increase | 8.0m shares approved, but 41.9% of votes cast were against. Plan terms and shareholder vote are disclosed; a post-vote Board response was not identified. | Amber |
| Delisting / going-private proposal | No such proposal identified in the reviewed filings. | Green, limited to reviewed filings |

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

| Dimension | Read | Evidence-based conclusion |
|---|---|---|
| Board oversight | Eight of nine elected directors are classified independent and the chair is independent. | **Favourable structure.** The Burger/BT relationship is a specific commercial-connection monitor, not a demonstrated independence breach. |
| Voice and accountability | Annual elections, majority voting, proxy access and a new 25% special-meeting right support holder voice. | **Adequate.** The 25% threshold and ban on written consent limit practical access for dispersed holders. |
| Change-of-control protections | Advance notice, blank-check preferred-stock authority, vacancy filling and Delaware Section 203 can impede an unsolicited offer. | **Constrained.** No conclusion is made that these tools will be misused. |
| RPT protection | Item 404(a) disclosure is favourable and a review policy is disclosed, but the proxy is outside the frozen set and no matched economic RPT data are available. | **Incomplete evidence.** No “no leakage” conclusion is supported. |
| Shareholder feedback | 41.9% opposition to the stock-plan share increase is a high-severity voting signal; 45.7% support for the political-spending report is also material. | **Requires monitoring.** The sources reviewed do not provide a Board response to either signal. |

**Overall protection read: adequate but constrained.** The structural safeguards are real, but the defence package and the contested stock-plan vote prevent a “strong” conclusion. The biggest evidence limitation remains the frozen pool’s missing proxy, ownership and comprehensive insider-data set. The governance triage specifies that the ownership-data gap caps shareholder friendliness at 60/100; that cap is applied below even though a public SEC proxy was reviewed as supplemental context. [Governance Data Triage, AKAM, 2026-09-14, pp. 11–12 and 19]

## Board & Shareholder Rights Score

| Component | Weight | Raw score | Rationale |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Board structure and independence | 20 | 17 | Independent chair and 8/9 classified independent directors; the two disclosed commercial connections prevent a fully clean independence read. |
| Process, renewal and succession | 15 | 11 | Meeting cadence, evaluation method and refreshment are disclosed; skills mapping, full overboarding and succession outcomes are not. |
| Committee effectiveness | 15 | 12 | Independent Audit Committee, financial expert and meaningful meeting cadence; post-AGM membership and evidence of challenge are incomplete. |
| RPT and minority protection | 20 | 10 | Favourable Item 404 statement and a review policy, but the proxy is outside the frozen pool and no matched RPT financial exposure is available. |
| Rights, corporate actions and AGM voting | 30 | 17 | Annual elections, proxy access and new special-meeting right are offset by written-consent limits, takeover defences and the 41.9% stock-plan opposition. |
| **Raw total** | **100** | **67** | Mixed / adequate structural protection, with material evidence gaps. |
| **Data-sufficiency cap** | — | **60** | Governance triage caps shareholder friendliness at 60 because the frozen pool lacks ownership and comprehensive insider data; the public proxy supplement does not change the frozen input inventory. |
| **Published score** | **100** | **60** | **Mixed. Confidence: 3/5.** |

**Red-flag status:** `RF-SHR-001` is triggered at **High** severity: 41.9% of votes cast opposed the 8.0m-share stock-plan increase. This is not a critical governance flag and does not itself establish misconduct or invalidate the investment. It does require a Board response, stock-plan burn-rate disclosure and a reassessment at the next annual meeting.

**Single highest-value next data request:** Add the filed 2026 DEF 14A and a complete ownership / Form 4 export to the frozen evidence generation. This one addition resolves the source-bound gaps in board composition, committee membership, Item 404, beneficial ownership, pledges, pay voting and shareholder rights.

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — AKAM

## 1. Owning the Misses

There is no completed management-guidance miss in the available record: Q2 revenue, non-GAAP EPS, and non-GAAP operating margin all landed within the Q1 ranges. The closest adverse communication event is the Q2 update to FY2026 guidance: the revenue high end fell by $20m, non-GAAP EPS high end by $0.10, and the operating-margin outlook moved from about 26% to 25%–26%. The new ranges were publicly reported, but the prepared remarks did not plainly label the changes as reductions or give a complete bridge; readers need to compare the two releases. [Management & Track Record, AKAM, §4; Q1 2026 earnings release, 2026-05-07, p. 2; Q2 2026 earnings release, 2026-08-06, pp. 1–2]

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| No completed Q2 2026 management-guide miss: revenue $1,099.682m, non-GAAP EPS $1.59, and non-GAAP operating margin 25% all fell within Q1 ranges | The Q2 release reported each measure and the call stated that the 25% non-GAAP operating margin was in line with management’s expectations. | Owned on the stated non-GAAP guide; this is not evidence of candor through an actual miss. | [Q1 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2; Q2 2026 earnings release, Results, p. 1; Q2 2026 earnings call, CFO prepared remarks, p. 6] |
| FY2026 guide narrowed after Q2 — not an actual miss | Q2 presented the new full-year ranges and gave individual drivers including LayerX’s expected $0.12 non-GAAP EPS dilution, lower FX benefit, and investment-led margin pressure. It did not expressly call the $20m revenue-high-end and $0.10 EPS-high-end changes reductions or reconcile the whole change. | Partly owned: the facts were disclosed, but the comparison and full bridge were left to the reader. | [Q1 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2; Q2 2026 earnings release, Financial guidance, p. 2; Q2 2026 earnings call, CFO prepared remarks, pp. 6–8] |
| Q2 2026 GAAP profitability decline — not a management-guide miss | The release disclosed GAAP operating income of $80m, down 47%, GAAP operating margin of 7%, down 8 percentage points, and GAAP diluted EPS of $0.52, down 27%. The call instead led profitability commentary with non-GAAP net income and margin. | Partly owned: reported in the release, but given less weight in spoken management framing. | [Q2 2026 earnings release, Results, pp. 1–2; Q2 2026 earnings call, CFO prepared remarks, p. 6] |

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | FY2025 non-GAAP results excluded $459.4m of stock-based compensation (SBC), exceeding FY2025 GAAP net income of $452.0m. Restructuring charges were $56.6m, $95.4m, and $58.1m in FY2023–FY2025; H1 2026 also included $275.0m of SBC and $2.0m of restructuring charges. | [Q2 2026 Supplemental Financial Information, GAAP to Non-GAAP Reconciliation sheet, FY2023–H1 2026 columns; Earnings Quality, AKAM, §§4–5] |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | In Q2 2026, GAAP operating income was $80.3m versus non-GAAP operating income of $270.7m: a $190.4m adjustment, or 237% of GAAP operating income. FY2025’s $686.7m operating-income adjustment was 121% of GAAP operating income ($1,253.6m non-GAAP versus $566.9m GAAP). | [Q2 2026 Supplemental Financial Information, GAAP to Non-GAAP Reconciliation sheet, Q2 2026 and FY2025 columns; Earnings Quality, AKAM, §§4, 7] |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | Yes. The reconciliation is disclosed, so the presentation is not hidden; however, it removes recurring employee pay, recurring restructuring, and acquired-intangible amortization. The Q2 release therefore showed non-GAAP EPS of $1.59 beside GAAP EPS of $0.52, a $1.07 difference. | [Q2 2026 earnings release, Results, pp. 1–2; Q2 2026 Supplemental Financial Information, Non-GAAP Definitions and GAAP to Non-GAAP Reconciliation sheets] |

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

The releases give usable decision data: GAAP and non-GAAP results are shown side by side, the reconciliation is itemized, and revenue is broken into Security, Delivery and other cloud applications, and Cloud Infrastructure Services (CIS). [Q2 2026 earnings release, 2026-08-06, pp. 1–2; Q2 2026 Supplemental Financial Information, GAAP to Non-GAAP Reconciliation sheet] The solution-category KPI is only newly reported from Q1 2026, although prior FY2025 quarters were recast; this improves visibility into CIS but does not establish five years of stable KPI reporting. There is no evidence in the available record that a KPI was dropped or redefined after deterioration, so this is an Amber comparability limit, not RF-MGT-003. [Q1 2026 Supplemental Financial Information, Supplemental Revenue sheet; Q2 2026 Supplemental Financial Information, Supplemental Revenue sheet]

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

Q2 is the weakest current period available: the release disclosed the GAAP earnings and margin decline while the CFO also directly named Delivery and other cloud applications revenue down 6%, higher colocation, depreciation and headcount costs, and an expected step-up in capex. [Q2 2026 earnings release, Results, pp. 1–2; Q2 2026 earnings call, CFO prepared remarks, pp. 6–8] The CEO’s opening was heavily promotional about AI contracts and third-party recognition, but in Q&A the CFO answered revenue timing, cost pass-through, pre-revenue colocation costs, and the GPU-delivery slip; notably, he said CIS growth should not accelerate in Q3 before a projected Q4 ramp. No direct numerical contradiction between the management commentary and the disclosed results was identified in the two available verbatim 2026 calls. [Q2 2026 earnings call, CEO prepared remarks, pp. 4–5; CFO Q&A, pp. 9–14]

## 5. Candor Read

**Mixed.** Management does put the adverse operating facts and revised ranges into the public record and gives direct, useful answers on the timing and cost of the CIS build-out. But the economic earnings picture is made materially better by excluding recurring SBC and repeat restructuring charges, while the prepared-message tone gives much more space to AI validation than to falling GAAP profitability.

The single biggest candor signal is the reconciliation itself: FY2025 SBC of $459.4m exceeded GAAP net income of $452.0m, and Q2 2026 non-GAAP operating income was $270.7m versus $80.3m on a GAAP basis. This is aggressive adjusted framing, not evidence that the disclosed GAAP figures are false. [Q2 2026 Supplemental Financial Information, GAAP to Non-GAAP Reconciliation sheet, FY2025 and Q2 2026 columns]

RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)

## 3B. Lineage / Phoenix Check

**Verdict: Green, confidence 3/5.** Akamai’s own history distinguishes pre-incorporation MIT research beginning in 1995 from the legal company founded on 20 August 1998. The SEC/frozen filing record is consistent with 1998 Delaware incorporation. No former legal name, predecessor company, dissolved prior issuer or suspicious asset/name migration was identified in the completed sources. This is not a state-registry-complete phoenix clearance. | Frozen-pool related-party disclosure | FY25 Form 10-K incorporates the proxy for Item 13; the proxy was absent from the frozen pool. | The frozen-pool gap remains open and limits reliance on cross-module related-party work. |
| Capital IQ relationship graph | Graph contained zero nodes, edges and disclosed relationships. | The graph’s scope is only recently disclosed supplier/customer relationships, not a complete related-party or group map. Zero rows cannot prove no related parties. |
| Capital IQ ownership fields | Insider ownership/activity fields were missing. | Ownership, pledges and transaction trend cannot be inferred as zero. |
| Person-roster mismatch | The public 2026 proxy names Ahola, Williams and Ford, while the frozen seed roster did not list them; the frozen source also names an unnamed Chief Security Officer. | The three named people were added to the register. The unnamed role remains an explicit gap. This is an evidence-inventory/reconciliation issue, not evidence of a company concealment. |

**Result:** No disclosable-and-omitted matter was identified. The absence result is limited by missing frozen proxy/ownership data, the empty vendor relationship export and incomplete registry depth.

## 5. Scope Boundaries

| Boundary | Why it matters | Effect on conclusion | Required next evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| FY26 proxy absent from frozen pool | Proxy carries Item 12/13 ownership, pledge, pay and related-party material incorporated by the FY25 10-K. | Caps confidence; public-EDGAR proxy read is a reconciliation aid, not a frozen-pool substitute. | Add the filed 2026 DEF 14A to the frozen generation. |
| Chief Security Officer unnamed | A key management role cannot be subject to person-specific checks without a name. | One potential KMP remains unswept. | Confirm name, start date and prior employer in an official filing or company disclosure. |
| Legal and court database coverage | Searches did not produce attributable material hits, but state corporate registries, state courts, FINRA/BrokerCheck, PACER and direct global-list records were not fully retrievable. | “Clean” grades are coverage-limited and cannot support a strong-clean conclusion. | Named database exports / official database result pages with stable identifiers. |
| External-directorship and entity network | The public proxy lists selected outside boards; no complete registry-based directorship map or charge-holder trail was obtained. | Related-entity and conflict conclusions remain partial. | Officer/director corporate registry and beneficial-owner mapping. |
| Trademark / brand ownership | Product branding and former LayerX branding were observed; USPTO and foreign trademark ownership were not verified. | Cannot rule out a brand-owner affiliate or licensing exposure. | Trademark-register and acquisition-IP schedules. |
| Noname legal-name mismatch | “Noname Gate Ltd.” and “Noname Security Ltd.” appear in source seeds without reconciliation. | Entity linkage is not assumed; no adverse conclusion. | Acquisition closing schedule and post-acquisition subsidiary ledger. |
| Group subsidiary population | FY25 Exhibit 21.1 contains 57 named entities. Only material/linked examples above were scoped. | No all-entity integrity clearance. | Group legal-entity ownership chart, directors and charge-holder records. |

## 6. Provenance & Next Request

### Source use

- **Tier 1 / primary frozen evidence:** FY25 Form 10-K, Q1/Q2 FY26 Forms 10-Q, including the FY25 Exhibit 21.1 subsidiary listing and the statement that Items 10–14 are incorporated from the proxy.
- **Tier 1 / primary public regulator sweep:** SEC EDGAR 2026 DEF 14A, filed 2026-03-31. Used for the current roster, disclosed outside roles and Item 404/family-relationship read. It is not represented as part of the frozen pool.
- **Company source:** Akamai’s company-history and company/IR pages. The history page was German original and translated faithfully for lineage; it is self-disclosed, so it is not used to prove legal registry facts.
- **Tier 5 vendor inputs:** CIQ facts sidecar and Capital IQ Suppliers/Customers relationship graph. The ownership export was missing; the relationship graph had zero rows and expressly does not cover the full group/related-party universe.
- **Public legal-database sweep:** SEC enforcement, DOJ, CourtListener and OpenSanctions scoped web queries. “No attributable hit identified” is a sweep-log result only; it is not proof of a clean record.

**Single highest-value next request:** Add the filed **2026 DEF 14A**, including its ownership/pledge, compensation, Item 404 and director/officer disclosure sections, to the frozen evidence generation; then provide a named KMP roster including the Chief Security Officer.

## 7. People & Network Integrity Read

**Verdict: Coverage-limited — no disqualifying or material personal-integrity fact identified from completed checks.**

The strongest positive fact is the public SEC proxy’s explicit statement of no 2025 Item 404(a) transactions and no family relationships among the named executive officers/directors, while Akamai has a separate independent chair. The key limitation is not a proven issue at a person or entity: it is the evidence boundary. The frozen pool omitted the proxy incorporated into the FY25 10-K, CIQ ownership data are missing, the relationship graph is empty and critical registry/court checks could not be completed. This caps the people-and-network score at 65/100 and confidence at 70/100.

**Monitor items:** (1) confirm Ahola’s Nasdaq council recusal/conflict controls; (2) reconcile Noname Gate Ltd. versus Noname Security Ltd.; (3) identify the unnamed Chief Security Officer; and (4) replace the coverage-limited public searches with identity-anchored official records.
